---
description: "Stellar developers discuss Jump Cannon asset interoperability, CAP-54's native token design, wrapping classic Stellar assets, account authorization, and CAP-52's unified host-function invocation model."
tags: rough
---

https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2NUs2Ojqw1E

The discussion focuses on asset interoperability between classic Stellar and Jump Cannon, the proposed smart contract runtime. Following the successful Protocol 19 upgrade, participants review CAP-54 as an alternative to CAP-48 and CAP-49. The proposal introduces a general-purpose native token contract that supports Stellar account authorization, token administration, and moving assets between classic Stellar and smart contracts.

Participants also examine how native tokens could support future parallel transaction processing, the trade-offs of extensible token functionality, and the implications of maintaining separate classic and smart balances. The conversation concludes with a review of CAP-52's proposed `InvokeHostFunction` operation, which would simplify contract interactions and introduce a unified mechanism for accessing supported host functions.

### Key Topics

- **Protocol 19 Upgrade:** The successful network upgrade introduces new transaction preconditions and signer functionality, supporting payment channels, blockchain bridges, and account recovery services.

- **Asset Interoperability Proposals:** CAP-48, CAP-49, and CAP-54 represent mutually exclusive approaches to connecting classic Stellar assets with Jump Cannon. CAP-54 proposes a more comprehensive native token implementation.

- **CAP-54 Token Interface:** The proposed contract includes standard token functionality such as balances, transfers, allowances, approvals, and metadata, alongside account-based authorization compatible with Stellar's multisignature capabilities.

- **Token Administration:** A flexible administrator interface would support minting, burning, and other supply controls. Administrators could be smart contracts, enabling applications such as liquidity pools.

- **Wrapping and Unwrapping Assets:** The `move_to_smart` and `move_to_classic` functions would transfer assets between classic Stellar and smart contracts while preserving existing authorization mechanisms. Participants discuss wallet complexity, exchange compatibility, destination accounts, and memo requirements.

- **Native Token Performance:** A standardized token implementation with predictable behavior could support future Speedex-like parallel transaction processing, while arbitrary custom contract hooks could introduce performance limitations.

- **Token Extensibility:** Participants consider configurable features, contract wrappers, and transfer hooks, weighing customization against the benefits of standardized native tokens.

- **Deployment and Issuer Requirements:** The CAP-54 deployment mechanism and administrator interface require additional feedback from asset issuers and further implementation discussion.

- **Unified Host-Function Invocation:** CAP-52 introduces an `InvokeHostFunction` operation to consolidate contract calls and related functionality, reducing the need for separate transaction operations.

- **Protocol Governance and Host Functions:** Participants discuss restricting externally callable host functions through an allowlist and applying appropriate protocol change controls to new functionality.

### Resources

- [CAP-0047: Core Advancement Proposal](https://github.com/stellar/stellar-protocol/blob/master/core/cap-0047.md)
- [CAP-0048: Asset Interoperability Proposal](https://github.com/stellar/stellar-protocol/blob/master/core/cap-0048.md)
- [CAP-0049: Asset Interoperability Proposal](https://github.com/stellar/stellar-protocol/blob/master/core/cap-0049.md)
- [CAP-0052: Smart Contract Authorization and Host Functions](https://github.com/stellar/stellar-protocol/blob/master/core/cap-0052.md)
- [CAP-0054: Native Token Contract and Asset Interoperability](https://github.com/stellar/stellar-protocol/blob/master/core/cap-0054.md)
